## Requirements Analysis - SMC Title 23 Finding Revision
**Timestamp**: 2026-08-19T15:50:00Z
**User Input**: "I want to refine the SMC Title 23 finding before we treat it as a major architectural red flag. The current Seattle Municipal Code Title 23 is freely accessible through Municode, and the City of Seattle itself explicitly directs users to Municode as its codifier for browsing and searching the current SMC... Our architecture does not require dynamically ingesting the municipal code or automatically converting it into production rules. The intended regulatory workflow is curated: authoritative legal source -> human/AI-assisted research -> structured rule specification -> deterministic implementation -> tests -> human source verification -> human approval -> production rule. Therefore, please distinguish these separate issues: 1. Whether the current law is publicly accessible for research. 2. Whether there is a supported API/bulk export suitable for automated ingestion. 3. Whether Municode's terms restrict scraping, copying, storing, or redistributing its hosted text. 4. Whether we can safely store citations/section identifiers and our own independently written rule representations without reproducing Municode's content. 5. Whether Seattle's underlying ordinances, City Clerk legislative records, SDCI materials, or other City-hosted sources can provide authoritative provenance and regulatory-change tracking independent of bulk Municode ingestion. Please research these distinctions and revise research-findings.md if appropriate. Do not assume that the absence of a Municode API is itself a blocker or major architectural problem... The important unresolved issue is what use of the underlying legal text and citations is legally and operationally appropriate for a commercial product, and what sources should constitute our authoritative provenance chain."
**AI Response**: "Researched the five distinctions via WebFetch/WebSearch: confirmed Seattle's City Clerk names Municode as official codifier (freely browsable); confirmed no official API exists (unchanged, but reframed as non-issue given curated workflow); found Municode's terms restrict their platform/website content to personal/non-commercial use; identified the government edicts doctrine (Georgia v. Public.Resource.Org, 2020 SCOTUS) establishing that legislative enactments/ordinances are not copyrightable, supporting that independently-written rule representations + citations are standard, low-risk practice, with one narrow open nuance (private codifier clickwrap terms) flagged for counsel rather than treated as a blocker; confirmed clerk.seattle.gov/seattle.legistar.com as independent city-run ordinance-level provenance/change-tracking sources, better suited than Municode for the regulatory-change-management requirement. Revised research-findings.md Section 1.7 (downgraded from 'RED FLAG' framing to a resolved, non-blocking finding with a recommended provenance chain) and Section 5 consolidated findings accordingly."
**Context**: Requirements Analysis stage, INCEPTION phase - artifact revision per user-directed research refinement; still awaiting clarifying-question answers (gate before Step 7).

AI must still NOT have authority to activate a regulatory rule. For rules that are straightforward and well-supported... I may perform the human verification and approval... ## Unit 2B - Functional Design: Questions Answered; Artifacts Generated
**Timestamp**: 2026-08-24T15:10:00Z
**User Input**: "[Answers to the 5 clarifying questions in unit-2b-commercial-payment-fulfillment-functional-design-plan.md, each with detailed reasoning beyond a bare letter choice:] Q1=A: refund state and report-access state remain separate; refunding a paid order does not automatically revoke report access for accuracy-dispute, goodwill, or other ordinary refunds where a real report exists (a generation-failure refund normally has no completed artifact/credential to revoke in the first place); preserve refund != report deletion, refund != access revocation as an architectural rule - EvidenceReportArtifact stays immutable, Report Access continues to own access credentials, and any future fraud/security-driven access revocation must be an explicit Report Access action, never an implicit side effect of OrderState becoming refunded. Q2=X: automatically INITIATE a full refund when a paid ReportGenerationJob reaches genuinely terminal FAILED, but model refund processing as its own real lifecycle rather than immediately declaring the order REFUNDED - existing per-call bounded retries and stale-IN_PROGRESS recovery are not the same thing as retrying a terminal FAILED job; once FAILED, Checkout & Fulfillment requests a refund through Order & Payment, Order & Payment records refund-initiated, Stripe is called with an idempotent refund request, the customer sees 'a full refund has been initiated' (not 'has been refunded'), and the order becomes REFUNDED only when Stripe confirms success (Stripe refunds can be pending/requires_action/succeeded/failed/canceled) - if the refund fails, preserve that explicitly, do not mark REFUNDED, and surface a support/manual-resolution requirement for Unit 3's future operator UI; the refund API call must be safe to retry using a stable Stripe idempotency key derived from the internal order/refund operation so a crash around the Stripe call cannot create duplicate refunds. Q3=A: build real transactional email delivery in Unit 2B since ACC-1 explicitly requires a secure emailed link and Unit 2B is not commercially complete without actually sending it; provider selection belongs in NFR Requirements; keep the flow Stripe Checkout -> verified webhook -> generation -> artifact COMPLETE -> create/obtain guest access credential -> email the secure link to the address Stripe Checkout collected; the success page must NOT be the durable access-delivery mechanism, only a status display ('payment received, report being generated' then 'report ready, check your email') - never expose a report merely because the browser reached Stripe's success URL, server-side order state remains authoritative; this also avoids weakening the hash-only bearer-token invariant to support two simultaneous delivery channels; email failure must not fail the generation job itself - model delivery failure separately and retry appropriately, and if a raw token was generated for an attempt whose delivery result is uncertain, rotating the credential before a resend is acceptable so only the newly-delivered link remains valid; do not persist the raw token merely to make retries easier. Q4=X: keep GenerationAuthorizationType.INTERNAL_PROTOTYPE as a supported internal domain capability but remove it from the ordinary public customer flow entirely - VERIFIED_PAYMENT becomes the ONLY customer-facing production path; /configure changes from configure->INTERNAL_PROTOTYPE->generation to configure->PO-0 readiness->create Order->Stripe Checkout->verified webhook->VERIFIED_PAYMENT->generation; keep INTERNAL_PROTOTYPE available for founder demos/test reports/comp'd reports/dev-integration testing but expose it only through an explicitly internal/operator mechanism - no public route or button may call authorizeReportGeneration with INTERNAL_PROTOTYPE since that would be a direct paywall bypass; no new authentication framework needs to be invented solely for this, an internal script/CLI/dev-only mechanism is sufficient for Unit 2B; the important invariant is public customer path -> VERIFIED_PAYMENT only, INTERNAL_PROTOTYPE never masquerades as a paid order. Q5=X: use BOTH a small processed-Stripe-event ledger AND existing domain/state-machine idempotency - Stripe explicitly recommends recording processed webhook event IDs since the same event can be delivered multiple times; persist a minimal ProcessedStripeEvent{stripeEventId (unique), eventType, stripeObjectId, processedAt}; webhook handling: verify signature -> parse event -> check ledger -> if already processed return success/no-op -> perform domain transition transactionally/idempotently -> record processed event -> return success; the ledger is NOT the only protection since Stripe notes two distinct Event objects may occasionally describe the same underlying condition, so also preserve domain safeguards: Order PAID transition is conditional/idempotent, VERIFIED_PAYMENT authorization created at most once per paid order, ReportGenerationJob remains idempotent, only one authoritative job may arise from one paid order, refund creation is idempotent, duplicate/out-of-order webhook delivery cannot move an Order backward; do not create a general event-sourcing architecture, just a small webhook-receipt ledger plus the already-approved domain-state protections. [Plus an ADDITIONAL FUNCTIONAL-DESIGN CORRECTION on Order state, submitted unprompted alongside the answers:] Do not use the originally-proposed PENDING->PAID->REFUNDED plus PAYMENT_FAILED and CANCELED as written - Stripe Checkout has no terminal 'payment failed' Checkout Session state corresponding cleanly to PAYMENT_FAILED, since a customer can fail a card attempt and still retry within an open Checkout Session, so a failed attempt must not prematurely terminalize the Order; a Checkout Session's actual lifecycle is approximately status:OPEN/COMPLETE/EXPIRED and payment_status:PAID/UNPAID/NO_PAYMENT_REQUIRED; for Unit 2B, prefer OrderState={PENDING,PAID,REFUND_PENDING,REFUNDED,REFUND_FAILED,EXPIRED} with exact semantics specified for each state (naming may vary); do not use GENERATING/COMPLETE here, those remain exclusively ReportGenerationJob state. [Plus explicit scope instructions:] keep Stripe Checkout deliberately simple and accept immediate card-based payment only for Unit 2B, do not enable delayed/asynchronous payment methods, but even with immediate methods the webhook handler must verify the Checkout Session/payment is actually paid before transitioning to PAID (do not transition merely because an event type named checkout.session.completed arrived) per Stripe's own fulfillment guidance requiring idempotent fulfillment logic that inspects Checkout's payment status, webhooks remain the reliable mechanism, the success landing page is not proof of payment. Keep the report price server-configured, $9.99 may remain the initial configured default but continue to document it as the current launch/pricing hypothesis not commercially validated demand, the browser never submits an authoritative price, the amount/currency persisted on Order is the exact amount used to create that Checkout Session and becomes part of the immutable commercial record. Use the email collected by Stripe Checkout as the guest-delivery address, persist the email required for fulfillment/support on the Order after it is obtained from verified Stripe-side data, do not trust a browser-returned email value as fulfillment identity. Functional Design should preserve explicitly: browser redirect is never payment proof; Stripe webhook signature is verified before event data is trusted; webhook verification uses the exact raw request body required by Stripe's signature-verification mechanism; invalid signatures produce no Order transition; client-supplied order/payment status is never trusted; Stripe identifiers are mapped back to an existing internal Order; price/amount is reconciled against the Order before PAID fulfillment. [Final instruction:] Proceed with Unit 2B Functional Design. Generate business-logic-model.md, business-rules.md, domain-entities.md. Do not broaden scope into accounts beyond ACC-1 guest mode, subscriptions, pricing tiers, coupons, enterprise billing, analytics infrastructure, marketing-site work, or Unit 3 admin tooling. Stop at the normal Functional Design review gate."

MAKE PAYMENT WEBHOOK LOCAL SIDE EFFECTS ATOMIC - for a verified successful-payment webhook, the local database changes that constitute fulfillment authorization must commit atomically within one DB transaction: verify current Order eligible for PENDING->PAID, transition to PAID/set paidAt, populate verified Stripe references and customerEmail, construct/persist VERIFIED_PAYMENT authorization, idempotently create the ReportGenerationJob, record ProcessedStripeEvent, then commit; do not commit PAID and the processed-event receipt while leaving job creation for a later non-atomic operation, otherwise a crash could create PAID + processed event + NO ReportGenerationJob and the replay would correctly no-op, permanently stranding a paying customer; external webhook-signature verification occurs before this transaction, report generation itself remains asynchronous and outside it. 5. ## Unit 2B - NFR Design APPROVED With Pattern 9 (Durable Fulfillment Reconciliation) Added
**Timestamp**: 2026-08-24T16:45:00Z
**User Input**: "Request one targeted NFR Design correction. The overall Unit 2B NFR Design is approved in substance. Do NOT reopen the existing patterns or architecture. Add one durable fulfillment-reconciliation pattern before proceeding to Infrastructure Design. MATERIAL GAP: post-job side effects are currently crash-sensitive - Unit 2B relies on transition-time hooks (ReportGenerationJob COMPLETE -> issue credential -> send email; ReportGenerationJob FAILED -> initiate automatic refund; Order REFUND_PENDING -> call Stripe refund API) that cross durable-state/external-side-effect boundaries, so a process crash can produce COMPLETE-with-no-email-initiated, FAILED+PAID-with-no-refund-initiated, or REFUND_PENDING-with-no-Stripe-call-ever-made - these states must be recoverable automatically, a paying customer must not depend on the exact process invocation that observed the terminal transition surviving long enough to perform the follow-up. ADD PATTERN 9: use existing persisted state as the source of truth; do NOT introduce a queue, outbox table, message broker, Redis, second worker service, or general workflow engine; at Unit 2B scale extend the existing in-process polling/reconciliation mechanism; reconcile at least three conditions with specific recovery behavior for each - (1) completed report needs delivery (issue credential if none exists, rotate-and-retry for stale EMAIL_PENDING or EMAIL_FAILED, mark EMAIL_SENT only on actual Resend acceptance, must survive restarts), (2) failed paid job needs refund (initiate the existing GENERATION_FAILURE process, PAID->REFUND_PENDING must stay conditional/idempotent so multiple passes can't create multiple logical refunds), (3) REFUND_PENDING needs Stripe submission/resumption (retry the SAME logical refund using the SAME refundIdempotencyKey, covering both the crash-before-Stripe-call and Stripe-accepted-but-response-lost cases, no new logical Refund if stripeRefundId already known, final REFUNDED/REFUND_FAILED still only from verified webhook). RECONCILIATION EXECUTION MODEL: prefer extending the existing Railway application's in-process poller rather than a new infrastructure primitive, either as an additional phase on the existing tick or a small sibling interval through the same lifecycle, preserving exactly-once poller startup, graceful shutdown, database-driven idempotency, and correctness that must not depend on one replica even though Unit 2B still runs one. CONCURRENCY: reconciliation operations must use conditional DB transitions/claims so repeated or overlapping runs are harmless, not relying on the single-replica deployment as the correctness mechanism. DELIVERY ATTEMPT RECOVERY: EMAIL_PENDING needs stale-attempt semantics similar to the existing ReportGenerationJob stale-claim recovery - when an EMAIL_PENDING attempt exceeds a configured uncertainty threshold, treat its outcome as uncertain, revoke/rotate the old credential, issue a new raw token, attempt a new email - intentionally accepting that the customer might receive an older email whose token was already revoked if the provider actually accepted the first send, judged safer than two live credentials. TESTING: add deterministic crash/reconciliation tests for all 6 specified scenarios (COMPLETE-then-crash-then-eventual-delivery; stale EMAIL_PENDING rotation; FAILED+paid-then-crash-then-refund-initiated; REFUND_PENDING-then-crash-then-resubmission-with-same-key; Stripe-accepted-but-lost-then-no-duplicate; repeated-ticks-produce-no-duplicates). GUEST STATUS CAPABILITY HARDENING: add to Pattern 4 - never log the full Checkout Session ID unnecessarily, never log a full success/status URL containing it, status endpoint response Cache-Control: no-store, success/status page Referrer-Policy: no-referrer, no third-party analytics scripts that could observe the full URL/session ID, status read remains enum-only/minimized, possession never authorizes report access or mutations - the Checkout Session ID does not need to be redesigned into another custom credential, these controls are sufficient for its deliberately narrow read-only scope. ## Platform Pivot APPROVED With Workflow-Start Idempotency Correction
**Timestamp**: 2026-08-24T18:05:00Z
**User Input**: "PLATFORM PIVOT REVIEW - ONE TARGETED CORRECTION. The Railway -> Vercel pivot is APPROVED IN SUBSTANCE. Do not reopen the platform decision or broader Unit 2B architecture. Before Code Generation, correct the Workflow-start idempotency design. MATERIAL CORRECTION: do not describe Vercel Workflow hook tokens/hook.getConflict() as providing an exactly-once or atomically-idempotent workflow start - current Vercel Workflow behavior allows start() to create a duplicate run before the duplicate discovers the hook conflict; hook-token conflict detection may remain as an optimization/in-flight duplicate suppression mechanism but must NOT be the correctness boundary - the Permit Preflight database remains the authoritative idempotency and execution-state boundary. REPORT GENERATION WORKFLOW: preserve the existing durable ReportGenerationJob state machine as the authoritative claim mechanism - verified authorization creates ReportGenerationJob exactly once in Neon, calls Vercel Workflow start(), the workflow's first side-effectful DB step attempts the existing atomic claim QUEUED->IN_PROGRESS; only the run that successfully claims may execute the pipeline; if another run starts for the same job its claim affects zero rows, it determines another run already owns/completed the job, and exits without running Property Intelligence/PostGIS/Rules Engine/Anthropic/artifact creation/delivery - multiple workflow runs may exist but only one may own the domain job, this is acceptable and the correct distributed-systems boundary. START()-FAILURE/LOST-RESPONSE: correctness must not depend on knowing whether start() succeeded; the reconciliation mechanism should inspect ReportGenerationJob state - if QUEUED and eligible beyond an appropriate window, call start() again; if the original workflow was actually started, whichever run reaches the atomic claim first wins, all others exit harmlessly; if IN_PROGRESS/COMPLETE/FAILED the reconciler does not start another ordinary execution; do not rely on Vercel's hook-token retention semantics for this decision. WORKFLOW RUN ID: a Vercel workflowRunId may be persisted for observability/support/debugging/linking to Vercel inspection but is NOT the authoritative identity of report execution - ReportGenerationJob.id remains the business/domain execution identity; do not require workflowRunId to be written successfully for job correctness. HOOK TOKEN: keep only as defense-in-depth (token approximately report-generation:{reportGenerationJobId}), a duplicate workflow may call hook.getConflict() and exit/adopt the existing run where appropriate, but document explicitly that start() itself may still create another run, the hook does not provide atomic admission, hook conflict protection is not the source of exactly-once domain behavior, database job claiming is authoritative. POST-COMPLETION DEDUPLICATION: do not assume Workflow hook tokens remember completed runs indefinitely, the database already does - if COMPLETE no workflow retry may produce a second EvidenceReportArtifact, if FAILED normal generation is terminal per the existing job model and the generation-failure refund path applies, a later Workflow start against either state exits without executing the pipeline. REFUND WORKFLOW: apply the same principle to processRefundWorkflow - Vercel Workflow start uniqueness is not authoritative, Order/refund state plus Stripe's stable refund idempotency key remain the correctness mechanisms, repeated refund Workflow starts must safely converge through conditional Order transitions/existing logical refund-attempt state/same Stripe idempotency key for retries of the same logical attempt/verified Stripe webhook confirmation - no duplicate refund may depend on a Workflow hook preventing a second run. WORKFLOW STEP RETRIES: assume Workflow steps are at-least-once side-effect execution, any side-effectful step must remain idempotent using existing DB conditional transitions/uniqueness constraints, Stripe idempotency keys, report artifact uniqueness/idempotency, access-credential rotation rules - stable step metadata/stepId may be forwarded as an external idempotency key where useful and where it matches the external API's semantics, but do not assume workflow durability means side effects execute exactly once. CRON RECONCILIATION: the 5-minute Vercel Cron backstop remains approved - because this project is on Vercel Pro, the required frequency is supported; Cron remains a safety/reconciliation mechanism detecting durable state that failed to progress (eligible QUEUED jobs whose start failed/was lost, completed reports lacking delivery, failed paid jobs lacking refund initiation, REFUND_PENDING operations needing safe resumption); Cron runs themselves must also be idempotent, overlapping invocations harmless. KEEP THE REST OF THE PIVOT (explicit list of everything to keep: Vercel canonical hosting, Vercel Workflows, Neon/Postgres/PostGIS as domain source of truth, neon-http for ordinary queries, request-scoped neon-serverless Pool for the transaction, Stripe, Resend, MapTiler, Anthropic, Vercel GitHub deployment/preview model, Vercel environment variables, @sparticuz/chromium+puppeteer-core, the deployed-PDF and real-Workflow verification items, low-frequency Cron, completed Unit 1/2 status) and do not reintroduce Railway/a persistent Node poller/Redis/queue broker/outbox/external workflow provider/separate worker hosting. DOCUMENTATION CORRECTION: anywhere the pivot currently claims deterministic hook tokens/hook.getConflict() provide 'idempotent starts,' change the terminology to something like 'in-flight Workflow duplicate detection/defense-in-depth' and identify ReportGenerationJob's atomic database claim as the authoritative execution-idempotency mechanism. After applying this targeted correction, consider the infrastructure pivot APPROVED and proceed to Code Generation. Do not create another infrastructure review gate unless implementation research discovers a genuine Vercel incompatibility."

Added a new 'Correction: Workflow-Start Idempotency' section to the ADR (aidlc-docs/decisions/2026-08-24-deployment-platform-pivot-railway-to-vercel.md) stating plainly that the first draft was wrong, explaining exactly why (start() not atomic with the hook-conflict check), and specifying the corrected authoritative boundary for both report generation (claimQueuedJob's existing atomic claim as the workflow's first step) and refunds (the conditional PAID->REFUND_PENDING transition) - also corrected the ADR's plan-tier framing from an assumed Hobby plan to the project's actual Vercel Pro plan (Workflow Data Retained 7 days on Pro, 1M req/min rate limit, actual included Events/Data-Written allowances need checking against the live dashboard rather than assumed from public Hobby figures). PAID - NEW OPERATOR REFUND - preserve current behavior (operator chooses CUSTOMER_REQUEST or GOODWILL plus required free-text justification; then validate operator, validate current Order state, persist REFUND_INITIATED audit entry, commit, start processRefundWorkflow; selected refundReason stored in audit metadata and passed to the Workflow exactly as currently designed). REFUND_PENDING - RESUME EXISTING LOGICAL REFUND - explicit instruction not to let the operator choose or replace the refund reason since this is not a new refund but a transport/workflow resumption of the SAME logical refund attempt; explicit instruction to use the already-persisted order.refundReason and existing order.refundIdempotencyKey through Unit 2B's unchanged processRefundWorkflow/processRefund behavior; conceptual UI description (Refund pending / Existing reason: CUSTOMER_REQUEST / required retry justification / Resume Refund Submission button) with the operator supplying only a new human justification; explicit statement that the audit entry may continue using REFUND_INITIATED since the approved vocabulary has no separate REFUND_RESUMED action and the entry means 'operator requested/initiated this refund command' not 'new logical refund created'; explicit instruction that its metadata must contain the EXISTING persisted refundReason, not a newly client-selected reason; explicit instruction that if a REFUND_PENDING Order somehow lacks a valid persisted refundReason or refundIdempotencyKey, treat that as an invariant problem and fail closed, never inventing either value. SYSTEM-INITIATED REFUND REASONS - explicit statement that a REFUND_PENDING refund may legitimately have originated from GENERATION_FAILURE or DUPLICATE_PAYMENT, which remain system-only reasons the operator is not selecting by resuming the workflow, so it is acceptable for the resume action to use an already-persisted system reason; explicit do-not-expose-GENERATION_FAILURE-or-DUPLICATE_PAYMENT-as-choices-when-originating-a-NEW-PAID-refund instruction. ADMIN UI - explicit rendering spec: PAID shows 'Initiate Refund' with a CUSTOMER_REQUEST/GOODWILL selector and required justification; REFUND_PENDING shows 'Resume Refund Submission' with the existing refund reason displayed read-only, required justification, no reason selector; REFUNDED/REFUND_FAILED/PENDING/EXPIRED show read-only state with no refund command button, preserving REFUND_FAILED's manual/support-resolution-only rule. AUTHORITATIVE STATE AFTER COMMAND - explicit instruction not to fabricate REFUNDED or claim Stripe has completed anything after a successful start() call, instead reloading the Order or otherwise refreshing the displayed authoritative Order state; explicit acknowledgment that it's acceptable if the immediate state is still PAID briefly after start() returns since Workflow execution is asynchronous; suggested UI copy 'Refund command accepted; confirmation is pending'; explicit statement that only the verified Stripe webhook may produce the confirmed terminal refund state. TESTS - explicit list of 10 required coverage items (PAID allows new CUSTOMER_REQUEST/GOODWILL refund command; REFUND_PENDING allows resumption; REFUND_PENDING resumption uses the EXISTING persisted refundReason; REFUND_PENDING resumption does not allow substituting a new refund reason; REFUND_PENDING still converges through Unit 2B's existing persisted refundIdempotencyKey; PENDING/REFUNDED/REFUND_FAILED/EXPIRED each reject before AdminActionLog/start(); the UI exposes the refund action for PAID and REFUND_PENDING only); explicit do-not-add-new-refund-states-workflow-or-infrastructure instruction. LOGICAL COMPONENT RESPONSIBILITIES in logical-components.md: Persistence/migration layer (preserves existing data, enforces workflow-shape integrity, owns schema rollout compatibility); Spatial Analysis/PostGIS adapter (owns geometry validation and computation, returns successful spatial results or explicit data-quality uncertainty, never performs regulatory classification itself); Report-generation orchestrator (maps data-quality uncertainty to the evaluator's fail-closed evidence path, routes true computation failures into existing job failure/retry behavior, preserves STAGE_TIMING instrumentation); Regulatory evaluator (consumes valid spatial facts/evidence status, does not catch infrastructure failures and convert them into regulatory uncertainty). ## Unit 6 Functional Design Part 2 Review — Founder: REQUEST CHANGES (four localized corrections)
**Timestamp**: 2026-08-27T11:00:00Z
**User Input**: "UNIT 6 FUNCTIONAL DESIGN PART 2 REVIEW. REQUEST CHANGES - FOUR LOCALIZED CORRECTIONS. The overall Unit 6 Functional Design is APPROVED IN SUBSTANCE. Do NOT reopen: magic-link/passwordless authentication choice; dual proof-of-control mechanisms; AccountOrderLink architecture; orderId UNIQUE anti-transfer invariant; immediate account deletion policy; retention of Order/EvidenceReportArtifact; guest access remaining independently valid; PC-3 deferral; ADM-9 deferral; minimal account scope. Apply ONLY the following corrections, then present Functional Design once more for final approval. 1. NEVER PUT RAW MAGIC-LINK TOKENS IN URL QUERY/PATH - frontend-components.md currently says /account/verify reads the token from the URL query string. Do not place a raw LOGIN or CLAIM_PURCHASE bearer token in query string, pathname, or redirect query parameters. Use a fragment-to-POST-body exchange: email URL is /account/verify#token=<raw-token>; browser reads location.hash, immediately removes the fragment from browser history, POSTs {token} in the request body to the verification endpoint; server hashes/resolves token, never logs or returns raw token. Apply the same pattern to CLAIM_PURCHASE magic links. The raw token should exist only in the email, browser fragment briefly, and verification POST body. Do not introduce a new crypto/token mechanism. 2. DEFINE REAL ACCOUNT-AUTHORIZED REPORT ACCESS - ACC-3 report history must actually let an authenticated account open its linked reports without possessing the original guest bearer token. Define two independent authorization modes for the SAME immutable report: A. GUEST ACCESS - existing reportAccessCredential bearer token -> existing guest authorization remains unchanged. B. ACCOUNT ACCESS - valid AccountSession + AccountOrderLink(accountId, orderId) + server-side resolution from Order to the immutable report artifact -> authorized account access. Account access must: obtain accountId only from the resolved AccountSession; verify the requested order belongs to that account through AccountOrderLink; resolve the report artifact server-side; never require Order.customerEmail equality; never reconstruct/recover the old guest raw token; never mint a replacement guest token merely to make Account History work; never revoke or alter the existing guest credential. Unauthorized account access should fail without revealing whether some other account owns the order. Update BR-U6-4, Workflow 4, domain/service responsibility language, and frontend report-history navigation accordingly. This is an additional authorization route to the same immutable report, not a second report-storage/rendering mechanism. 3. ACCOUNT DELETION MUST INVALIDATE LOGIN TOKENS TOO - current deletion only removes MagicLinkToken rows where accountId matches. But LOGIN tokens deliberately carry no accountId. Therefore account deletion must capture the deleting Account's normalized email and invalidate/delete: all AccountSession rows for accountId; all CLAIM_PURCHASE MagicLinkToken rows referencing accountId; all outstanding LOGIN MagicLinkToken rows for that normalized account email; all AccountOrderLink rows for accountId; the Account row. Order/EvidenceReportArtifact/reportAccessCredentials remain untouched. This prevents a pre-deletion LOGIN link from recreating the account after deletion without a newly-requested authentication link. HTTPONLY COOKIE CORRECTION - ACCOUNT_SESSION_COOKIE is HttpOnly. Client-side JavaScript cannot clear an HttpOnly cookie. Logout and account deletion must clear/expire it in the SERVER RESPONSE using the project's shared cookie helper/Set-Cookie semantics. Frontend code reacts to successful logout/deletion and redirects; it does not directly mutate the HttpOnly cookie. 4. MAKE TOKEN CONSUMPTION RACE-SAFE AND ATOMIC - the design currently claims the first verifier sets consumedAt and concurrent verifiers lose the race. Make that an explicit persistence invariant. Token consumption must be a conditional atomic operation equivalent to: UPDATE magic_link_tokens SET consumed_at = now() WHERE token_hash = ? AND purpose = ? AND consumed_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now() RETURNING ...; exactly one concurrent verifier may receive the successful row; unknown/expired/consumed/losing-race outcomes remain externally indistinguishable. LOGIN TRANSACTION: for LOGIN verification, perform in one DB transaction: atomically consume LOGIN token; find/create Account for normalized email; create AccountSession. If the transaction fails, it rolls back the token consumption too. Do not leave a valid authentication token permanently consumed without successfully establishing the corresponding account/session. CLAIM_PURCHASE: use the same atomic token-consumption primitive for CLAIM_PURCHASE. Then perform the AccountOrderLink operation using the existing orderId UNIQUE race-safe invariant. If concurrent link creation encounters the unique constraint, resolve the authoritative existing link: same account -> idempotent success; different account -> ALREADY_LINKED_TO_ANOTHER_ACCOUNT. Do not rely on a pre-check alone. NEXT: update domain-entities.md, business-rules.md, business-logic-model.md, frontend-components.md. Do not expand the account feature set. Present Functional Design for one final review. Expected result after these four corrections: Functional Design APPROVED -> NFR Requirements."

(3) Corrected deleteAccount to capture the account's own normalized email before deleting the Account row, then delete outstanding LOGIN tokens by that email in addition to CLAIM_PURCHASE tokens by accountId - added as a new explicit step with the exact reasoning (LOGIN tokens carry no accountId by BR-U6-1's own discriminated shape) documented inline; applied the HttpOnly-cookie correction (server-side Set-Cookie clearing, never client JS) to both logout and deletion across domain-entities.md, business-rules.md, business-logic-model.md, and frontend-components.md. ## Unit 6 Functional Design — APPROVED/COMPLETE; NFR Requirements Scope Directed
**Timestamp**: 2026-08-27T11:30:00Z
**User Input**: "UNIT 6 OPTIONAL ACCOUNTS - FUNCTIONAL DESIGN REVIEW. APPROVED / COMPLETE. All four requested corrections are accepted. Do not reopen Functional Design. Proceed directly to NFR Requirements. NFR REQUIREMENTS SCOPE: Perform a TARGETED, DELTA-ONLY Unit 6 NFR Requirements pass. Unit 6 introduces genuinely new NFR surfaces: 1. customer authentication/session security, 2. magic-link bearer-token security, 3. account authorization/IDOR resistance, 4. concurrency and transactional integrity, 5. account deletion/privacy behavior, 6. auth-endpoint abuse resistance. Do not rewrite unrelated project-wide NFRs. 1. MAGIC-LINK/TOKEN SECURITY: cryptographically high-entropy tokens using the existing token primitive; hash-only token persistence; LOGIN and CLAIM_PURCHASE tokens short-lived and single-use; purpose separation (LOGIN token cannot satisfy CLAIM_PURCHASE and vice versa); raw tokens never persisted or logged; raw tokens never placed in URL path/query; fragment -> POST-body exchange; fragment removed from browser history promptly; unknown/expired/consumed/losing-race tokens externally indistinguishable; token issuance and verification responses must not create account-enumeration or token-validity oracles. 2. CUSTOMER SESSION SECURITY: separate session token from magic-link token; HttpOnly; Secure in deployed environments; appropriate SameSite behavior; server-side validation on every authenticated request; expiry enforcement; explicit logout revocation; deletion revokes all sessions; server-side Set-Cookie expiration on logout/deletion; session fixation prevention; authentication state must never be inferred from cookie presence alone. 3. AUTHORIZATION/DATA ISOLATION: accountId always resolved from AccountSession, never client authority; AccountOrderLink authoritative for account-owned report access; Order.customerEmail never an authorization mechanism; account report access cannot enumerate orders/reports belonging to another account; unauthorized requests do not reveal whether the target Order/report exists; guest bearer-token access remains independently functional; Account Access does not mint/recover/reconstruct guest credentials; deletion of an Account cannot alter retained report/order authorization semantics for the independent guest-token path. 4. CONCURRENCY/TRANSACTIONAL INTEGRITY: token consumption atomic; only one concurrent verifier can consume a token; LOGIN consume + Account find/create + session creation transactional; failure rolls back token consumption; Account.email uniqueness race-safe; AccountOrderLink.orderId uniqueness authoritative anti-transfer enforcement; concurrent same-account link attempts resolve idempotently; concurrent different-account attempts produce one owner and one deterministic rejection; account deletion transactional so partial unlink/delete states cannot remain. 5. PRIVACY/DELETION: confirmed account deletion deletes Account, revokes/deletes sessions, invalidates account-bound CLAIM_PURCHASE tokens, invalidates outstanding LOGIN tokens for the normalized account email, removes AccountOrderLink records, does not mutate Order, does not mutate EvidenceReportArtifact, does not revoke independent guest report-access credentials, does not claim Order.customerEmail is deleted; account deletion must not leave account-specific authentication material usable afterward. 6. AUTH-ENDPOINT ABUSE RESISTANCE: Unit 6 adds publicly reachable email-triggering auth endpoints; define a bounded abuse-resistance requirement for repeated magic-link requests, repeated verification attempts, claim-email triggering; prefer reuse of existing platform/IP-level controls where sufficient; do not introduce Redis, a distributed rate-limit service, CAPTCHA, or a new security vendor unless requirements demonstrably cannot be satisfied without one; do not invent precise numeric limits without grounding them in existing platform capabilities or later Build & Test evidence. 7. EMAIL DELIVERY/FAILURE BEHAVIOR: distinguish token successfully persisted, email provider accepted the message, email delivery actually reaching the user; do not treat Resend acceptance as proof of inbox delivery; failures must not expose whether an Account or Order exists; avoid leaving unusable/authentication-corrupting state when email submission fails; define expected retry/re-request behavior. 8. PERFORMANCE/RETENTION: keep performance requirements modest - session resolution/account-history queries must use indexed server-side lookups; AccountOrderLink history must not require unbounded application-side scans; expired/revoked/consumed authentication records must have an operationally manageable cleanup/retention posture; do not invent a large-scale identity architecture. 9. INHERITED BASELINE: explicitly inherit unchanged where appropriate - Vercel deployment topology, Neon/Postgres, Resend provider, report-generation reliability, payment/refund system, report immutability/provenance, admin Basic Auth/CSRF, backup/recovery baseline, accessibility baseline; Customer Account authentication is separate from Unit 3 admin authentication. NEXT: Present the targeted Unit 6 NFR Requirements for review. Do not proceed to NFR Design yet."

Wrote nfr-requirements.md with 50 numbered requirements (NFR-U6-1 through NFR-U6-50) across the 9 founder-specified sections, grounding every reuse claim in real existing code read directly: report-access/credential.ts's generateAccessCredential/hashToken/resolveByAccessToken for token security (section 1) and session security (section 2); shared/cookies.ts's existing HttpOnly/Secure/SameSite=Lax convention and cookie-is-a-transport-not-a-trust-boundary philosophy; AccountOrderLink's real database-level UNIQUE constraint for concurrency/anti-transfer enforcement (section 4), specifying the exact insert-then-resolve-conflict resolution logic; BR-U6-5's delete/retain split restated as explicit NFR-level requirements (section 5), closing the same LOGIN-token-by-email gap Functional Design review had found; read report-access/rate-limiter.ts and shared/rate-limiter-instance.ts in full to confirm FailedLookupRateLimiter is a real, generic, already-approved, source-keyed (x-forwarded-for via the real sourceKeyFor helper in app/api/reports/route.ts), bounded-memory, self-cleaning primitive - reused unchanged for verification-endpoint abuse resistance (its existing failure-counting mode) and extended with one small, explicitly-flagged every-attempt-counts variant for the two email-triggering endpoints (section 6), reusing the exact existing DEFAULT_RATE_LIMITER_OPTIONS numbers rather than inventing new ones, per instruction; read email-delivery/resend-client.ts to confirm and reuse Unit 2B's own already-established EMAIL_SENT-means-accepted-not-delivered semantics unchanged for section 7; kept section 8's performance/retention requirements deliberately modest (existing UNIQUE-constraint-backed indexed lookups, no new index design, no purge job introduced); wrote section 9's explicit inherited-baseline list including a structural-separation statement between Unit 6's customer AccountSession and Unit 3's admin Basic Auth. Unauthenticated request-login endpoints do not become customer-session CSRF operations merely because they send email. Magic-link verification is authorized by possession of its single-use bearer credential; do not invent an unrelated CSRF requirement around the raw token exchange unless the implementation also mutates an authenticated session. 2. PRIMARY/OUTER ABUSE CONTROL: use Vercel's existing platform Firewall rate-limiting capability for the public auth/email-triggering endpoints - no new vendor, Redis instance, database, or application service; protect at least the request-login-link endpoint, claim-purchase email-trigger endpoint, and where useful verification endpoints; do not invent new numeric limits if the existing 10/60s/60s prototype limit is being intentionally reused, record it as initial tuning subject to real deployment evidence. APPLICATION-LOCAL DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH: FailedLookupRateLimiter may remain for invalid bearer-token attempts, local defense-in-depth, deterministic tests, but must NOT be described as the authoritative deployment-wide email-bombing control on Vercel. No Redis, no CAPTCHA, no new rate-limit vendor. 3. MAKE UNIQUENESS-RACE HANDLING TRANSACTION-SAFE - NFR-U6-28 currently says a concurrent accounts.email INSERT loses the UNIQUE race and then re-resolves the existing account inside the same LOGIN transaction; a raw PostgreSQL UNIQUE violation aborts the transaction until rollback or savepoint, so that sequence cannot be relied upon literally. Require a transaction-safe conflict strategy - acceptable examples: INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING ... then SELECT the authoritative existing Account, or explicit SAVEPOINT/nested transaction around the conflicting INSERT. Binding requirements remain: accounts.email UNIQUE is authoritative, exactly one Account exists for normalized email, concurrent valid login flows may both establish independent sessions, the losing account-create race does not abort a legitimate LOGIN transaction merely because another transaction created the Account first. Apply the same principle to AccountOrderLink conflict handling whenever the claim operation is running inside a transaction - do not assume code can catch a raw UNIQUE violation and continue using an already-aborted PostgreSQL transaction. 4. REQUIRE THE ACCOUNT-HISTORY INDEX THAT NFR-U6-47 ASSUMES - NFR-U6-47 describes report history as an indexed WHERE account_order_links.account_id = ? query; the current Functional Design schema defines orderId UNIQUE but does not create an index on accountId; Postgres does not automatically index a merely foreign-key-shaped column. NON-BLOCKING SECURITY WORDING CLEANUP: correct NFR-U6-9's statement that the atomic UPDATE is inherently 'uniform-cost' - do not promise exact timing uniformity from PostgreSQL/cache/index execution; instead require one common externally-visible failure result, no deliberately different control-flow response based on unknown vs expired vs consumed vs race-lost, no intentional timing oracle; exact timing equalization is not required unless evidence later demonstrates a practical side channel. EVERYTHING ELSE APPROVED - keep unchanged: 256-bit existing token primitive, hash-only persistence, fragment->POST transport, purpose separation, atomic single-use consumption, independent AccountSession token, server-side session validation, accountId from session only, AccountOrderLink authorization, guest access independence, deletion semantics, LOGIN/claim transactional integrity, email-delivery honesty, existing Resend provider, no general identity platform, explicit baseline inheritance. NEXT: after these edits, mark Unit 6 NFR Requirements APPROVED/COMPLETE, update aidlc-state.md and audit.md, proceed directly to targeted Unit 6 NFR Design, do not hold another NFR Requirements review gate."

**Timestamp**: 2026-08-27T13:00:00Z
**User Input**: "UNIT 6 NFR DESIGN REVIEW. REQUEST CHANGES - ONE SECURITY CORRECTION ONLY. Everything else in Unit 6 NFR Design is APPROVED. Do NOT reopen: token generation/hashing, LOGIN magic-link design, fragment-to-POST transport, AccountSession design, shared same-origin extraction, ON CONFLICT conflict handling, AccountOrderLink uniqueness, report authorization design, deletion semantics, Vercel Firewall design, local rate-limiter defense-in-depth, Functional Design generally. Apply only the following correction, then mark NFR Design APPROVED/COMPLETE and proceed DIRECTLY to Infrastructure Design. Do not create another NFR Design review gate. CLAIM_PURCHASE EMAIL COMPLETION MUST BE BOUND TO THE AUTHENTICATED ACCOUNT THAT STARTED IT - the current design treats completeClaimByEmail(rawToken) as authorized solely by possession of the CLAIM_PURCHASE bearer token; that is insufficient. A CLAIM_PURCHASE token already contains the accountId of the authenticated Account that initiated the claim. Completion must therefore require BOTH: 1. a valid CLAIM_PURCHASE MagicLinkToken, 2. a valid AccountSession whose accountId exactly matches MagicLinkToken.accountId. The email token proves 'this browser/user controls the purchase email'; the AccountSession proves 'this browser/user controls the Account receiving the purchase'. Both proofs are required before AccountOrderLink may be created. ATOMIC CONSUMPTION: make the CLAIM_PURCHASE atomic-consumption statement account-bound - UPDATE magic_link_tokens SET consumed_at = now() WHERE token_hash = ? AND purpose = 'CLAIM_PURCHASE' AND account_id = <session-resolved-account-id> AND consumed_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now() RETURNING order_id, account_id, ...; accountId is NEVER client supplied, it comes only from the validated AccountSession. A valid claim token presented under no AccountSession, an expired/revoked AccountSession, or a different Account's session must NOT create a link. Do not reveal whether the failure was token validity, session mismatch, expiry, or another authorization condition beyond the minimum UI behavior needed to tell an unauthenticated customer to restart/sign in. CLAIM COMPLETION TRANSACTION: perform CLAIM_PURCHASE completion transactionally - 1. resolve the authenticated AccountSession, 2. atomically consume the CLAIM_PURCHASE token while requiring token.accountId == session.accountId, 3. insert AccountOrderLink using the already-approved ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING + SELECT strategy. If the link operation encounters an infrastructure/database failure, rollback token consumption so a valid claim link is not permanently burned without having established the corresponding AccountOrderLink. The existing outcomes remain: same account already owns order -> idempotent success; different account owns order -> ALREADY_LINKED_TO_ANOTHER_ACCOUNT. No automatic transfer. CSRF: because completeClaimByEmail now depends on AccountSession cookie authentication and mutates account state, add it to Pattern 3's account-side same-origin CSRF-protected mutation set, so the protected customer mutations become at least claimPurchase Path A start, claimPurchase Path B, completeClaimByEmail, logout, deleteAccount. The CLAIM_PURCHASE bearer token remains an additional authorization factor; CSRF protection does not replace it. FRONTEND BEHAVIOR: the claim-verification landing page must not silently complete a claim while the browser is unauthenticated or authenticated as a different Account. For this MVP, it is acceptable to fail closed with instructions equivalent to 'Open this verification link in the browser/account where you started the claim, or sign in and request a new verification email.' Do not build cross-device claim-transfer/session-handoff infrastructure. EVERYTHING ELSE APPROVED. After this localized amendment: mark Unit 6 NFR Design APPROVED/COMPLETE, update aidlc-state.md and audit.md, proceed directly to Infrastructure Design, Infrastructure Design SHOULD RUN not skip because the Vercel Firewall rule is a genuine new deployment-configuration surface, do not hold another NFR Design review."

## Live Bug Report — "Nothing happens" clicking Find Parcel — Found and Fixed
**Timestamp**: 2026-08-27T15:30:00Z
**User Input**: "Now nothing happens at all when I click the find parcel button"
**AI Response**: "Did not guess - attached this session's Browser tool directly to the founder's own real running local dev server (localhost:3000, already running on port 3000) rather than starting a separate instance, and reproduced the exact click live. Confirmed via read_network_requests that POST /api/parcels/resolve DID fire and returned a real 200 OK with live King County geocode data (CLARIFICATION_REQUIRED/INSUFFICIENT_CORROBORATION, one real candidate, parcel 1959703080, '3216 FUHRMAN AVE E, Seattle, WA, 98102') - the fix's own logic was executing correctly. A screenshot then revealed the real cause: the confirmation UI WAS rendering (visible as a very faint bounding box) but its text was almost completely illegible - dark-gray-on-black. Used javascript_tool to inspect computed styles and confirmed body/html backgroundColor were both transparent (rgba(0,0,0,0)) while body text color was the app's existing #1a1a1a (near-black, from app/layout.tsx, present since this app's very first commit) - with the browser's own prefers-color-scheme reporting dark (matches.true), the viewer/browser painted a black canvas behind the transparent page, rendering near-black text on a near-black background. This is a real, pre-existing bug this session's own new confirmation UI simply exposed for the first time (every prior page's content was mostly form controls, which render with a light system UI regardless of page theme, masking the same underlying issue). Fixed app/layout.tsx: added color-scheme: light to <html> and an explicit white backgroundColor to <body>, matching the light-theme styling every page's own inline styles (#1a1a1a text, #ddd borders) already assumed but never enforced. Re-verified live in the same browser session after the fix: reloaded /configure, confirmed the page now renders with a white background and legible dark text via screenshot, repeated the exact same address-search click sequence, confirmed the confirmation UI is now clearly legible, then clicked 'Yes, this is the property' and confirmed it correctly proceeds to the boundary-fetch step - which fails only on the separate, explicitly out-of-scope 'DATABASE_URL is not set' condition (no database configured in this local environment; not touched, per the founder's own explicit prior instruction not to work on DATABASE_URL). Ran npm run typecheck (0 errors) to confirm the layout.tsx edit didn't break anything. Updated the amendment doc's addendum, aidlc-state.md is not modified further for this narrow follow-on fix (recorded here and in the tracker instead, matching this correction's own established documentation location), and external-verification-tracker.md item 18 updated from 'not yet re-run' to 'partially verified - one real address, live browser + live King County data' with the full finding."
**Context**: A genuine, real regression report investigated with real tools against the real running server rather than assumed/guessed at from static code reading alone - the actual root cause (a pre-existing, latent dark-mode/transparent-background bug this session's own new UI happened to be the first content substantial enough to expose) was found via live network inspection, a screenshot, and computed-style inspection, then fixed and re-verified live in the same browser session, not merely asserted fixed from source-code reasoning.
